# Audit coverage manifest

[System](COMPLETE_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md) · [Capabilities](COMPLETE_CAPABILITY_INVENTORY.md) · [Traces](END_TO_END_EXECUTION_TRACES.md) · [Diagrams](ARCHITECTURE_DIAGRAMS.md) · [Findings](FINDINGS_AND_RECOMMENDATIONS.md) · [Coverage](AUDIT_COVERAGE_MANIFEST.md)

## Read this before interpreting coverage

**The tracked-file inventory is complete. The requested exhaustive line-by-line engineering audit is not complete.** All tracked files were byte-read and hashed; Python and JavaScript/TypeScript were structurally parsed. That is not equivalent to semantic inspection, complete control-flow tracing, or verification of every feature. This manifest deliberately distinguishes those activities. No report in this set is a production-readiness certification.

Every original tracked path appears exactly once in the inventory below. `R` means the complete source text was inspected; it does not establish all interactions or prove absence of defects. `T` means selected portions were traced or source was retrieved during investigation, with incomplete semantic coverage. Retrieval ranges include tool-output batches that were truncated, so **they are not claimed as line-by-line coverage**. `I` means indexed only and substantive semantic inspection remains outstanding. All `I` implementation files and unreviewed portions of every `T` implementation file are expressly unresolved; they are not exclusions.

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| R | Complete source-text semantic read recorded; behavior verification separately reported. |
| T | Trace/retrieval evidence exists, but complete semantic read is not established. |
| I | Byte inventory and applicable syntax/definition/import indexing only; semantic review outstanding. |
| X-V | Individual content review excluded: licensed bundled TradingView charting-library vendor asset. Integration wrappers remain in scope. |
| X-B | Individual text review excluded: binary image/font/icon/certificate-like asset classified by inventory; identity retained. Packaging/configuration references remain in scope. |

## Boundaries and reproducibility

The boundary is the Git checkout, not the whole sandbox. Baseline was clean. No additional repository was cloned and no prior external architecture was used to define the system. No knowledge-base directory or repository knowledge guidance was present at the discovered locations. Root `AGENTS.md` is accounted for. Ignored installed dependencies, `.git`, build outputs, caches, local runtime state and environment secrets are excluded from first-party line review. Their generated/vendor nature, and the absence of authority to inspect production state, are the reasons. They are not counted as tracked first-party implementation. No secret values are reproduced.

The six new documents are audit outputs and are not part of the 1,990-file baseline. Existing source and configuration hashes were compared after the builds/probes. The manifest's hashes identify tested working-tree inputs, including every lockfile and configuration. Hashes are SHA-256 over raw bytes. Line counts for text follow the inventory newline convention; binary entries show a dash. The audit used Python 3.14.4, Node 24.21.0 and pnpm 11.25.0; these are local check versions, not claims about all supported deployments.

## Outstanding semantic scope

The per-file inventory is the exact outstanding list: filter status `I` for wholly unexamined semantic content, and `T` for incompletely examined content. This includes substantial frontend components, contracts, database lifecycle operations, adapter implementations, model-context processing, styles/locales, embedded CI test code, documentation, and most tests. Static symbol catalogs in the capability report account for their existence; they must not be mistaken for complete behavioral analysis.

No backend/PostgreSQL, live model, broker, external channel, signed native binary, real device, or live voice integration was verified. Excluded vendor internals were not security audited. Dynamic runtime introspection of FastAPI/Pydantic registries was unavailable because the Python application environment is absent. Source-level route expansion is provided instead. Runtime resources (prompts, policy manifests, skills, playbooks) are first-party inputs and remain in scope, including where indexed but not semantically reviewed.
